- Push: tuned MPM / DEM A = 1.37 (0.5 m/s, 6 cm), 1.42 (0.25 m/s), 1.32 (3 cm); task settings 1.11, 1.04, 1.09. Case P-a repeats round 4 on three other beds (round 4: 1.34 and 1.09).
- Pressing: the peak force while the target moves down is 2.27, 2.05 and 2.05 times the DEM value with the tuned grid and 0.43, 0.40 and 0.32 times at task settings (the first version quoted the first case only). The MPM box trails its target by 8 to 19 mm while pressing, so this peak is taken before the box is at depth; including the hold check 5 finds 2.2 to 2.4 times DEM for the tuned grid. At the end of the hold the tuned box still carries about 2000 N; DEM relaxes. The tuned sand is about twice as stiff as DEM A under a vertical load and the task-setting sand less than half as stiff, while in the push both are 4 to 42% above DEM A.
- Twist: each simulator's torque output is listed, but the two were not shown to mean the same thing, so no ratio is given.

**Paper figure (corrected after checks 5 and 6).** `~/isaac/dem_compare/results/paper/figures/t8_mpm_dem_stats.pdf` (single column, 3.45 x 1.75 in, vector; PNG preview and `t8_mpm_dem_stats_numbers.json` beside it; script `analysis/paper_stats.py`). Earlier versions, not for use: `t8_mpm_dem_stats_superseded.pdf` (first version, with the artefacts) and `t8_mpm_dem_stats_before_check6.pdf` (rim next to the box, push-up dispersion over all cases). Tuned MPM only; three groups, each a plain percent error against DEM; one bar per motion in the order horizontal push, push down, push up, twist:
- **penetration:** trench depth of the push and residual pit depth of the push upward; for the push downward and the twist the place of the bar reads "n/a (depth prescribed)";
- **deformation:** error of the berm or rim height taken one cell away from the box (cells 14 to 30 cm from the box centre; berm 16 to 40 cm from the centre line). Chosen over the rim next to the box because that one hinges, in the twist, on the single ring of cells swept by the box corners, where one case with a 0.7 cm DEM rim gives 117% (117.5%) and would set the axis; the corner excess (0.2 to 0.8 cm) is stated in the text instead. This measure was added after check 6 and is not part of the list fixed before the runs;
- **dispersion:** displacement error; for the push upward from the two 6 cm cases only.
- For the caption: bar = mean over all pairs of one MPM run and one DEM seed; error bar = spread within a case (mean over the cases of the standard deviation across the 9 pairs of a case); white dots = the means of the individual cases (speed and depth); hatched bar = the same error between DEM runs that differ only in the seed.
- Values: rows 1, 2 and 4 of the table above.

**Version 3 of the paper figure (requested by Junnosuke, 2026-11-24; no new analysis).** `results/paper/figures/t8_mpm_dem_stats_v3.pdf`, 3.45 x 1.95 in (0.2 in taller than before, to make room for the schematic; drawn with `analysis/paper_stats.py --v3`). Changes against `t8_mpm_dem_stats.pdf`: (1) a drawn schematic in the upper left, not data: a cross-section of the sand with the box lifted out of the pit it made, marking penetration (depth of the trench or pit), deformation (height of the berm or rim beside it) and dispersion (displacement of the sand, arrows on sand parcels); (2) the hatched DEM seed-against-seed bars and their legend entry are removed; the values stay in the table above; (3) the empty penetration slots of the push downward and the twist read "n/a (box stays in the sand)". The bars, error bars and dots are the same numbers. The version with the DEM level is kept unchanged as `t8_mpm_dem_stats.pdf` and also as `t8_mpm_dem_stats_with_dem_level.pdf`. Both are on the slides (P7).

**Version 4 of the paper figure (Junnosuke's corrections, 2026-11-24; labels only, no data change).** `results/paper/figures/t8_mpm_dem_stats_v4.pdf`, 3.45 x 1.75 in (`analysis/paper_stats.py --v4`); version 3 is untouched. The schematic carries one short label per marked thing ("box", "trench depth", "rim height", "sand displacement"); the group labels are "penetration (trench depth)", "deformation (rim height)", "dispersion (sand displacement)"; the 60% grid line stops under the schematic and the y axis ends at 70% with ticks to 60. In the figure "trench" stands for the trench of the push and the pit left after the push upward, and "rim" for the berm of the push and the rim of the other motions; the definitions above are unchanged. On the slides (P7) beside versions 3 and the one with the DEM level.

**Penetration for all four motions: mean depth of the depressed region (requested by Junnosuke; definition fixed on 2026-11-24 at 17:45, before it was computed; analysis only).** His reasoning: even where the motion of the box is prescribed, the depressed region can differ because the sand moves.
- *Measure.* Per run, at the end of the motion: the mean, over all 4 cm cells whose surface lies more than **1 cm** below its level before the box was lowered, of that drop. The cells around the box are included.
- *Under the box.* Where the box is still in the sand at the end (push downward, twist, and the end of the horizontal push), the surface of a cell that lies wholly under the box is taken as the box underside: its drop is the depth of the underside below the mass-equivalent surface as reached in that run (from the saved box position). This is what excluding the particles inside the box volume gives in both simulators, and it needs no particle dump. Cells cut by a box face are left out; that happens only at the end of the horizontal push (cells within 0.14 m of the commanded end position and of the centre line that are not wholly under both boxes).
- *Comparison.* Tuned MPM against DEM: |MPM - DEM| / DEM in percent for every pair of one MPM run and one DEM seed, and DEM seed against DEM seed as reference (in this file, not in the figure); absolute depths in centimetres.
- *Whether it is informative* is reported with it: the share of the depressed cells that lie under the box, and the same measure without those cells.
- *Rule for the figure.* The measure is used for all four motions if, for the horizontal push and the push upward, it gives the same percent error as the current measures (trench depth, residual pit depth) within a few percent; otherwise the current measures are kept for those two and the new one is used only for the push downward and the twist.

**Result (unchecked).** Tuned MPM against DEM A at 640 kg/m3, mean over the three cases of each motion:

| Mean depth of the depressed region | Horizontal push | Push downward | Push upward | Twist |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Error against DEM [%] (DEM seed against seed) | 4 (2) | 20 (4) | 16 (5) | 18 (2) |
| Depth [cm], MPM against DEM | 3.83 / 4.00 | 4.94 / 4.07 | 3.22 / 3.24 | 4.61 / 3.88 |
| Depressed cells per run, MPM / DEM | 134 / 167 | 25 / 33 | 25 / 41 | 28 / 38 |
| Share of the depressed cells that lie under the box [%], MPM / DEM | 12 / 10 | 100 / 76 | 0 / 0 | 91 / 66 |
| Without the cells under the box: depth [cm], MPM / DEM | 3.69 / 3.90 | none in MPM / 1.30 (8 cells) | same as above (no box) | 1.66 (3 cells) / 1.79 (13 cells) |
| Without the cells under the box: error [%] (DEM seeds) | 5 (2) | not defined: no MPM cell outside the box drops by more than 1 cm (6 between DEM seeds) | 16 (5) | 7 (4), from 18 of 27 pairs (MPM runs with fewer than 3 such cells left out) |
| Current measure for comparison: trench or residual pit depth error [%] | 7 (3) | | 25 (4) | |

- **Is the number informative for the push downward and the twist? Mostly not: it reports the box position.** In tuned MPM 100% (push downward) and 91% (twist) of the depressed cells lie under the box, where the depth is the prescribed one (within 0.7 mm in both simulators); in DEM 76% and 66%. The errors of 20% and 18% do not come from a different depth anywhere: they come from the number of shallow cells around the box that enter the mean. DEM has 8 (push downward) and 13 (twist) cells next to the box that drop by more than 1 cm, 1.3 and 1.8 cm deep, which pull its mean down; tuned MPM has 0 and 3. So the number says that DEM shows a shallow depression in the ring next to the box and tuned MPM does not. Check 6 found that ring to read 0.5 to 0.9 cm lower in every DEM seed because a grain centre cannot come within 7.5 mm of the box face, which is the same size as the 1 cm threshold; how much of the DEM depression is sand that sank and how much is this reading bias cannot be separated with these maps. Outside the box the twist cells that do drop agree (1.66 against 1.79 cm).
- **Horizontal push and push upward.** The new measure gives 4% for the push (current trench depth: 7%) and 16% for the push upward (current residual pit depth: 25%). The push agrees within a few percent; the push upward does not (16 against 25%: the fixed footprint of the current measure includes cells that the new one drops in MPM and keeps in DEM, 25 against 41 cells). By the rule fixed above, the current measures are kept for the push and the push upward, and the new one is used only for the push downward and the twist.
- **Task settings** (for the record): 4 / 13 / 23 / 32% against DEM at 733 kg/m3 (DEM seeds 1 / 5 / 6 / 4%).
- **Figure, version 5 (unchecked):** `results/paper/figures/t8_mpm_dem_stats_v5.pdf`, 3.45 x 1.75 in, same labels as version 4 (`analysis/paper_stats.py --v5`; version 4 untouched). The two penetration slots that read "n/a" now hold bars for the push downward (20%) and the twist (18%) with the new measure. They are drawn pale, because they are a different measure from the two solid penetration bars and mostly report the prescribed box position; a caption has to say so.

Figures: `site/T8/fig/r5_push.png`, `r5_down.png`, `r5_up.png`, `r5_twist.png` (the maps are drawn with the box cells; in the MPM panels of the push downward and the twist the cells inside the box outline show the artefact described above).

### P8. Fixed-weight press: the box free to sink under a set load (round 6, website only; plan fixed before the runs)

Fixed on 2026-11-24 at 17:48, before any run of this test. Requested by Junnosuke through the lead. In rounds 1 to 5 the motion of the box is prescribed, so its depth is an input. Here the box is free vertically and pressed by a set load, so the sinkage is an outcome of each simulator.

**Test.** The same box (0.2 x 0.2 x 0.1 m, 10 kg), on the bed of the stamp cases (1.0 x 1.0 x 0.25 m, box at the centre). The box is placed with its underside at the mass-equivalent surface and held there for 0.5 s. Then it is released vertically: horizontal position and rotation stay held, the vertical motion is free. The **total vertical load** on the sand (weight of the box plus an added vertical force, which is upward for loads below the weight of 98.1 N) rises from zero to its value in 10 equal steps of 0.05 s (0.5 s in all) and is then held for 1.5 s, so the load acts for 2.0 s from the start of the ramp in both simulators; the particles are saved at the end of it.
- DEM: the box is a dynamic body (not kinematic): x, y and the rotation are prescribed to zero without dictating the motion, z is free; the added force enters as a prescribed acceleration of the box.
- MPM: the vertical slide of the gantry has no position drive (stiffness and damping zero). Before the release the driver holds the box with its own position controller through the joint force; after the release the joint force is the added force alone. Horizontal slides: gantry 3e6 N/m as in round 5. Target and force are updated every physics step.

**Loads:** 50, 150, 350, 700 N total vertical load (written before the runs: "700 N is of the order of the load under one foot of a 70 kg robot standing on one leg"; check 7: that is about force, not pressure, and the G1 is 35 kg; see the foot scale in the results).

**Runs:** 4 loads x 3 repeats x 2 settings in each simulator = 24 MPM runs and 24 DEM runs. MPM: task settings (3 cm) and tuned (1 cm, 8 particles per cell), three beds (jitter seeds 42, 1, 2 with the bed shifted by 0, 1/4, 1/2 voxel). DEM: material A, 15 mm grains, at 733 and 640 kg/m3, seeds 42, 43, 44.

**Measures** (fixed before the runs):
- *Sinkage against time:* depth of the box underside below the mass-equivalent surface measured before the box touched the sand (same rule in both), every 0.02 s.
- *Settled sinkage:* its mean over the last 0.2 s of the load. MPM against DEM as |MPM - DEM| / DEM, with DEM seed against seed.
- *Time to settle:* the time after the start of the ramp from which the sinkage stays within 5% of the settled value (and at least within 0.5 mm).
- *Force balance at rest (check that the box is free):* mean vertical force of the sand on the box over the last 0.2 s (MPM: over all physics steps) against the load; it should agree within a few percent. If it does not, the test is not valid for that simulator and that is reported.
- *Rim height and sand displacement:* as in round 5, from the particles before the box touched the sand and at the end: rim = 95th percentile of the height change of the cells 0.1 to 0.3 m from the box centre (and 0.14 to 0.3 m); displacement error on 4 cm cubes relative to the DEM displacement.
- Statistics: mean and standard deviation over the three beds or seeds per load; pairs of one MPM run and one DEM seed for the errors.

**Results of round 6 (checked with caveats by check 7, whose corrections are applied here).** All 48 runs completed (`t8-mpm-r6-task-1`, `t8-mpm-r6-tuned-1`, `t8-dem-r6-d733-1`, `t8-dem-r6-d640-1`, 12 tasks each; tests `t8-mpm-r6-test-1`, `t8-dem-r6-test-1`; field reduction `t8-ana-g-wk-1`, `t8-ana-g-wn-1`, `t8-ana-g-d733-1`, `t8-ana-g-d640-1`); runs missing from the analysis: none. Script `analysis/round6.py`, numbers in `results/round6.json`.

Free to sink, the box goes 14 times deeper in task-setting MPM than in DEM at 350 N (6.8 against 0.5 cm) because that sand compacts under the box; tuned MPM holds 700 N with 0.6 cm where DEM A gives way (4.8 cm), the known strength mismatch of the two materials.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| tuned, 1 cm, 700 N | 0.60 ± 0.06 | 4.77 ± 0.19 | 0.13 ± 0.01 | 0.02 ± 0.02 | 0.00 ± 0.00 | 0.93 ± 0.37 | 0.53 ± 0.02 | 0.00 ± 0.00 | 0.00 ± 0.00 | 0.21 ± 0.16 | 0.95 ± 0.11 |

**Corrections after check 7 (2026-11-24; no new run).**
- *As run, against the plan.* In MPM the load rose in 0.5 s and acted for 2.0 s, as planned. In DEM each of the 10 load steps lasted 0.04 s, not 0.05 s (the DEM script rounds a phase to whole 0.02 s force windows), so the load rose in 0.4 s and acted for 1.9 s. This does not change the DEM sinkage (the box is at rest long before the end). The DEM zero (the mass-equivalent surface) lies 6.5 mm below the grain tops, so lowering the box to it presses the top grains before the release: the force in the hold reaches 35 to 77 N in single 0.02 s windows and relaxes to 0.2 to 9 N by its end. The DEM bed has therefore been pressed with up to the 50 N load and half the 150 N load before the measurement begins, which makes the ratios at 50 and 150 N generous to DEM; it does not matter at 350 and 700 N.
- *"Still sinking".* The first version quoted the mean of 1.5 to 2.0 s minus the mean of 1.0 to 1.5 s. The sinkage gained in the last 0.5 s is 0.11 / 0.19 / 0.34 / 0.12 cm at task settings (not 0.14 / 0.23 / 0.49 / 0.19 cm).
- *"Gives way" for task settings is replaced by "compacts"*, and the tuned result at 700 N is no longer called an error of MPM; see below.

- **The box is free in both simulators.** The vertical force of the sand on the box at rest equals the load within 0.11% in all 48 runs (MPM: mean over all physics steps of the last 0.2 s; DEM: mean of the last 10 samples of 0.02 s), and the box stays within 0.01 mm of its horizontal position. Check 7 verified the free motion from the box's acceleration as well. In MPM the vertical slide has no position drive; the joint force is the added force alone after the release.
- **Scale of the loads.** 50 / 150 / 350 / 700 N on the 0.04 m2 box are 1.25 / 3.75 / 8.75 / 17.5 kPa. A G1 sole (0.17 x 0.07 m, 343 N) is about 14 kPa in double stance, which corresponds to about 580 N on this box, and about 29 kPa in single stance, about 1150 N, which was not tested (check 7). For cohesionless sand equal pressure is not equal severity: a 7 cm wide sole is about three times closer to failure than the 20 cm box at the same pressure.
- **DEM material A.** Sinkage 0.10 / 0.21 / 0.47 / 3.38 cm at 50 / 150 / 350 / 700 N at 733 kg/m3 and 0.07 / 0.22 / 0.53 / 4.77 cm at 640 kg/m3, after the pre-press described above. It gives way between 350 and 700 N. That is where a bearing-capacity estimate puts a 32° sand of this density under this box: 520 to 840 N at 733 kg/m3 and 455 to 730 N at 640 kg/m3 by the formulas of Vesic, Terzaghi and Meyerhof (check 7; not recomputed here).
- **Tuned MPM (1 cm)** sinks 0.06 / 0.09 / 0.18 / 0.60 cm: 0.80 / 0.40 / 0.34 / 0.13 of the DEM sinkage. It holds 700 N where DEM A sinks 8 times deeper. **This is not an error of MPM:** 700 N is below the estimated failure load of a 36° to 40° sand (0.85 to 3.4 kN, check 7), the preset's friction angle is 36° and the tuned sand slides at 40° in the tilt test, while DEM A slides at 32.5°. The "8 times" is the known strength mismatch between the tuned sand and DEM material A; a load of 1500 to 3000 N would be needed to find where the tuned sand gives way. The sinkage rises with the load and still creeps 0.02 cm in the last 0.5 s at 700 N, so the sand responds. It is not converged in the voxel: the one test run at 2 cm sinks 0.28 cm at 350 N against 0.18 cm at 1 cm.
- **Task-setting MPM (3 cm)** sinks 1.64 / 3.46 / 6.81 / 9.49 cm: 17 / 17 / 14 / 2.8 times the DEM sinkage. **It compacts; it does not fail in shear.** From the particle dumps of one run at 350 N (check 7): under the box the thickness of the bed goes from 20.6 to 14.1 cm and the mean density rises by 26% (about 730 to 920 kg/m3), 14% of the mass goes sideways, and nothing rises around the box (the ring 0.14 to 0.3 m from the centre drops 0.3 to 0.6 cm; rim -0.17 cm). In DEM at 700 N the ring rises: constant volume. The sinkage happens at 6 to 70% of the estimated bearing capacity, so no friction angle reproduces it.
  - *The bed is not at rest.* The task-setting bed is generated at 640 kg/m3, has compacted to 724 to 734 kg/m3 in the 2 s before the test and goes on: the surface far from the box drops 0.37 to 0.46 cm over the run, the same at 50 and 700 N (check 7). **About 0.4 cm of each task-setting sinkage is the bed settling by itself**, about half of it before the release; without it the ratios are about 13 / 14 / 14 at 50 / 150 / 350 N.
  - *Contact at release.* On two of the three task-setting beds the box is not yet touching the sand at the release, because the surface has dropped since it was measured. Against the adjacent surface the 1.64 cm at 50 N is about 1.2 cm.
  - *Not at rest at the end.* In the last 0.5 s the box still sinks by 0.11 / 0.19 / 0.34 / 0.12 cm (DEM: under 0.001 cm), so the sinkages are lower bounds and the "time to settle" of the table is not a settling time for them. At 700 N the sinkage of 9.5 cm is about the height of the box.
  - Which of particle sampling, iteration limit and time step causes the compaction under load was not separated; round 2 showed that with 1.6 or more particles per cell edge the bed no longer compacts while settling.
- **Rim and displacement.** Rim height: tuned 0.21 against 0.95 cm at 700 N, task settings -0.14 against 0.81 cm (no rim in task-setting MPM at any load); at 50 to 350 N the DEM rim is under 0.15 cm. The displacement error relative to DEM is 93% (tuned) and 163% (task) at 700 N against 21% and 26% between DEM seeds; at the lighter loads too little sand moves in DEM for the measure to mean anything. With sinkages that differ by factors of 3 to 17, an error near or above 100% says only that the two boxes are in different places.
- **Limits.** One body, three repeats, DEM material A only (15 mm grains, not validated against experiment, not matched to the tuned sand in the tilt test), 2.0 s under load, one tuned voxel. Not for the paper figure.

Figures: `site/T8/fig/r6_sinkage_load.png`, `r6_sinkage_time.png`.

### P9. Which training setting makes the sand compact under load? (round 7, part A; website only; plan fixed before the runs)

Fixed on 2026-11-25 at 01:23, before any run of this round. Overnight task from Junnosuke through the lead; he was not available for questions, so the choices are written here.

**Question.** At task settings (voxel 3 cm, 1.25 particles per cell edge with a fixed jitter of 5 cm, step 5 ms, 25 iterations, tolerance 1e-4) the box sinks 13 to 17 times deeper than in DEM under a fixed load, by compaction (P8). Which of these settings causes it, and what is the cheapest setting at which it stops?

**Test.** The fixed-weight press of P8 (same box, bed 1.0 x 1.0 x 0.25 m, load rising in 0.5 s and held 1.5 s), at 150, 350 and 700 N, MPM only; DEM A of P8 is the reference. Stage 1: one setting changed from the task settings, one bed (jitter seed 42, grid unshifted), one run per load:

| Case | Changed from the task settings |
|---|---|
| T | nothing (task settings) |
| S16 | 1.6 particles per cell edge, jitter 5 cm |
| S20 | 2 particles per cell edge, jitter 5 cm |
| J05 | 1.25 per edge, jitter half a particle spacing |
| S20J05 | 2 per edge, jitter half a spacing |
| S20J10 | 2 per edge, each particle at random in its own sub-cell (jitter one spacing; the sampling of the tuned setting) |
| I100 | iteration limit 100 |
| I250 | iteration limit 250 |
| I250T5 | iteration limit 250, tolerance 1e-5 |
| D25 | step 2.5 ms |
| D125 | step 1.25 ms |
| V2 | voxel 2 cm, same sampling (1.25 per edge, jitter 5 cm) |
| GIMP | particle integration scheme "gimp" in place of "pic" (the one solver option found that changes how particle volume is spread over the grid) |

Solver and material options that bear on volume change, and why they are or are not varied: `integration_scheme` (pic or gimp): varied (case GIMP). `critical_fraction` (0 in the task: the fraction below which the yield surface collapses): not varied, raising it removes strength from loose regions and cannot stop compaction. Material `yield_pressure` (1e15 Pa in the preset, effectively no compaction by the material law), `hardening` (0), `dilatancy` (0): not varied; the preset already forbids compaction in the material law, so the compaction seen is numerical, and these are set in the scene and not reachable by a run-time override of the solver configuration.

Stage 2: the settings of stage 1 that stop the compaction, alone and combined with each other if no single one does, on three beds (jitter seeds 42, 1, 2 with the bed shifted by 0, 1/4, 1/2 voxel) at 150 and 350 N, together with the task settings on the same three beds. Stage 3: at the chosen setting, the four prescribed motions of P7 (cases P-a, S-a for push downward and upward, T-a; three beds) against the DEM runs of P7 at the matching bed density, with the push force.

**Measures.** Sinkage against time and at the end of the load; sinkage gained in the last 0.5 s; mean density of the sand column under the box (the 16 cubes of 4 cm under the footprint, |x|, |y| < 0.08 m; mass per area over the height from the floor to the surface before, and to the box underside at the end) before and at the end; rim height (95th percentile of the cells 0.1 to 0.3 m from the centre); the bed's own settling away from the box (median height change of the cells more than 0.3 m from the centre, in x or y, from before the box is lowered to the end); cost: number of particles and computing time per 20 ms of simulated time on one L40S.

**Reading, fixed before the runs.** A setting "stops the compaction" if, at both 150 and 350 N: (a) the bed settles by less than 0.1 cm by itself away from the box; (b) the column density under the box rises by less than 5%; (c) the box sinks by less than 0.05 cm in the last 0.5 s; and (d) the sinkage is below 1.5 cm at 350 N (DEM A: 0.5 cm; tuned 1 cm: 0.2 cm; task settings: 6.8 cm). Among the settings that pass, the cheapest is the one with the lowest computing time per 20 ms of simulated time; the answer is that setting and its cost relative to the task settings. If none passes, the answer is the list of what each setting changes. Stage 3 then asks two things of the chosen setting: is a rim built (rim height within the range between half and twice the DEM value in the push and the twist), and does the push force stay within 15% of DEM A (task settings: +9 to +11%).

**Results of round 7, part A (checked with caveats by check 8, whose wording changes are applied here).** Runs: `t8-mpm-r7-a1-1` (stage 1, 39 tasks, of which 8 failed right after the environment was set up, with no output and no error text; rerun in `t8-mpm-r7-a1b-1`), `t8-mpm-r7-a2-1` (stage 2, 57 tasks), `t8-mpm-r7-motions-1` (stage 3, 18 tasks), all at priority LOW; 96 press runs analysed. The particle dumps of these 3 cm runs are small, so they were downloaded to `data/r7_*` and reduced locally with `analysis/fields.py` (the reduction jobs `t8-ana-h-*` waited in the queue and were cancelled by me, except `t8-ana-h-a1-1`, which completed and is not used). Scripts `analysis/round7.py` (`results/round7.json`) and `analysis/round5.py --settings task tuned s20 s20j10 --cases Pa Da Ua Ta` (`results/round7_motions.json`).

Departures from the plan, made while the runs were queued: stage 2 and stage 3 were submitted from a preview of stage 1 (sinkage from the small files), before the density and settling measures were computed, to use the night; 1.8 and 2.5 particles per edge were added to stage 2 to locate the threshold; stage 3 was run for both 2-per-edge samplings (jitter 5 cm and random in the sub-cell) before it was known which passes.

**Corrections after check 8 (2026-11-25; no new press run).**
- *Cause, worded as the evidence supports.* The sparse, widely jittered particle sampling is the cause: it is the only setting whose change alone stops the bed settling by itself, and it cuts the sinkage six to sevenfold and the density rise under the box about tenfold at 150 and 350 N (fourfold in sinkage at 700 N). Iteration limit, time step and voxel size, **each changed alone on one bed**, leave the sinkage within the bed-to-bed scatter of the task settings (6.4 to 7.4 cm at 350 N on three beds). They were not combined with each other at the task sampling, and no run is converged in the iterations: the step time scales with the limit (19 / 42 / 87 / 88 ms), so the solver uses every iteration allowed and "250 iterations with tolerance 1e-5" is not a separate test. The gimp scheme is a **partial effect**, not "no change": 5.0 cm at 350 N and 2.3 cm at 150 N, outside the bed scatter; it is itself a change of how particle volume is spread over the grid, so it supports the sampling reading.
- *Density rise.* The values in the table below take the column height from the mass-equivalent surface measured before the box was lowered. Check 8 recomputed them with the surface measured at the release and finds 16 to 29% for the non-sampling cases (gimp 16.0% against 9.4% here, 250 iterations 25.4% against 19.3%): different in size, the same in reading.
- *Mechanism (check 8, from the particle dumps).* As generated, the task sampling leaves 11% of the 3 cm cells empty and 25% with one particle; after settling, before the load, 5% are still empty and 21% hold one. With 2 per edge at random in the sub-cell no interior cell is empty (8.2 particles per cell). The 5 cm jitter also throws particles out of the container: before the load 5.1% of the task-setting particles are below the floor plane and 4.1% outside the walls.
- *The reading.* "Every bed must pass" is in `analysis/round7.py` and was added after stage 1; it is not in the reading written before the runs. It is the stricter choice. Under it 2 per edge with the 5 cm jitter fails (on one bed: 1.75 cm at 350 N and 0.07 cm in the last 0.5 s); **on the means it passes all four criteria**. The answer does not depend on the rule: the sub-cell sampling is the cheaper of the two either way, by 4% (23.6 against 24.6 ms), about 1.5 standard deviations.
- *"Stops the compaction" is relative.* At the chosen setting the column still loses 4.4% of its height at 350 N while 1.9% of its mass leaves sideways and the density rises 2.6%: about 60% of the remaining sinkage is densification (check 8). At 700 N the density rise is 5.0%, at the limit of the reading, which was applied at 150 and 350 N only.
- *Against DEM: not a matched sand.* The denser sampling is twice too soft at 150 and 350 N (0.47 and 1.12 cm against 0.22 and 0.53 cm in DEM A; the absolute differences are below one grain and half a voxel), and at 700 N it holds (2.25 against 4.77 cm), as a 36° sand should (P8).
- *Force.* MPM / DEM A in the push: 1.11 at task settings, 1.69 (5 cm jitter) and 1.93 (sub-cell) at 3 cm with 8 particles per cell (2.08 in the round-4 series at 3 cm, which also used 250 iterations), 1.37 at 1 cm in these round-5 runs (1.34 was the round-4 value). **The 10% at task settings is a cancellation of two errors, not agreement:** a 3 cm grid overestimates the push force about twofold, and the task-setting sand compacts ahead of the box, which lowers it again. Statements in this file that present +4 to +11% at task settings as agreement with DEM (P6, P7) are to be read with this.
- *Cost.* 1.23 times the step time holds for one environment of this bed, where 92% of the task-setting step is fixed overhead (check 8: about 17.7 ms plus 0.077 ms per 1,000 particles); memory rises about 0.3 GB per bed. With many environments the part that scales with the particles dominates and the ratio moves towards 3.9; that is an extrapolation. With more environments it was measured afterwards (next paragraph).

**Cost with more environments (optional measurement suggested by the lead after check 8; unchecked).** Workflows `t8-mpm-r7-envs-1` and `t8-mpm-r7-envsb-1` (priority LOW; the 64-environment run with the dense sampling failed at setup the first time, with no error text, and completed on resubmission). The probe's fixed bed with every particle simulated, 1.6 x 1.6 m per environment (the size of the task's sand pool), at the streaming task's own sand settings (voxel 4 cm, depth of the task), sand at rest with the box hovering, 2 s; step time as the mean over 75 control steps of 20 ms, memory as the peak of the process on the GPU; one run each on an L40S (47 GB). Task sampling (1.25 per edge, jitter 5 cm) against 2 per edge at random in the sub-cells:

| Environments | Particles, task sampling | Particles, 2 per edge in sub-cells | Step time, task sampling [ms per 20 ms] | 2 per edge [ms] | Ratio | GPU memory of the process, task sampling [GB] | 2 per edge [GB] | Ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 20,000 | 83,200 | 16.6 | 18.1 | 1.10 | 1.1 | 1.5 | 1.33 |
| 16 | 320,000 | 1,331,200 | 52.7 | 174.0 | 3.30 | 3.5 | 8.5 | 2.44 |
| 32 | 640,000 | 2,662,400 | 115.9 | 367.7 | 3.17 | 5.9 | 15.7 | 2.64 |
| 64 | 1,280,000 | 5,324,800 | 267.6 | 793.2 | 2.96 | 10.8 | 30.2 | 2.80 |

- **With 16 to 64 environments the denser sampling costs 3.0 to 3.3 times the step time and 2.4 to 2.8 times the GPU memory**, against 1.10 times the step time with one environment: the ratio of 1.23 found on the single test bed does not carry over. 64 environments with the dense sampling take 30 GB and 793 ms per control step.
- This is not the training configuration: the training task simulates only the sand near the robot and keeps the rest in a particle cache, and it uses more environments. The measurement gives the scaling with the number of simulated particles, which is what both configurations share; the cost in the training task itself was not measured. The voxel here is the task default of 4 cm, not the 3 cm of the press test.

The sparse, widely jittered particle sampling makes the sand compact: with 2 particles per cell edge placed at random in their sub-cells the box sinks 1.1 cm at 350 N instead of 6.8 cm and the bed is at rest, for 3.9 times the particles and 1.23 times the computing time in one environment (about 3 times with 16 to 64 environments); more iterations, a smaller time step or a finer voxel, each alone, do not stop it.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 2 per edge random in sub-cell and 250 iterations | 1 | 0.35 | 0.60 | 1.38 | 0.01 | 1.2 | 1.6 | 0.01 | 0.25 | 76,313 | 44 | 2.29 | yes |

"Density under the box": rise of the mean density of the sand column under the box, end against before. "Bed settles by itself": drop of the surface more than 0.3 m from the box centre over the run. Sinkages below zero mean that the bed surface rose after it was measured (the 2 and 2.5 per edge beds with the 5 cm jitter swell slightly while settling). Mean ± standard deviation over the beds where three were run.

- **What causes the compaction: the particle sampling.** With one setting changed at a time, 100 or 250 iterations, a tolerance of 1e-5, a step of 2.5 or 1.25 ms, a 2 cm voxel and the gimp integration scheme all leave the sinkage at 350 N between 5.0 and 6.9 cm (task settings 6.8 cm), the density rise under the box between 9 and 26%, and the bed settling by itself. More particles per cell edge change it step by step: 3.83 cm at 1.6, 2.58 cm at 1.8, 1.44 cm at 2 and 0.02 cm at 2.5 per edge (350 N). The jitter matters as well: half a spacing at 1.25 per edge halves the sinkage (3.29 cm) but gives a looser bed (572 kg/m3), which still densifies under the box.
- **The cheapest setting that passes the reading: 2 particles per cell edge, each placed at random in its own sub-cell** (8 per cell; everything else as in the task: voxel 3 cm, 5 ms, 25 iterations). On three beds: sinkage 0.47 / 1.12 / 2.25 cm at 150 / 350 / 700 N (task settings 3.40 / 6.77 / 9.48 cm; DEM A at 640 kg/m3 0.22 / 0.53 / 4.77 cm; tuned 1 cm 0.09 / 0.18 / 0.60 cm), density under the box +2.8% at 350 N (task settings +27%), 0.01 cm in the last 0.5 s, the bed at 640 kg/m3 and at rest (0.01 cm of own settling against 0.42 cm). **Cost relative to task settings: 3.9 times the particles and 1.23 times the computing time per step** (24 against 19 ms per 20 ms of simulated time for one environment of this bed on an L40S; the cost in a training run with many environments and the particle cache was not measured).
- **Near misses.** 2 per edge with the task's 5 cm jitter fails two of the four criteria narrowly on at least one bed (1.44 cm at 350 N against the limit of 1.5 cm; 0.03 cm in the last 0.5 s) and sinks 3.7 cm at 700 N; 1.8 per edge still compacts (+4.7% density, 2.6 cm). 2.5 per edge passes (0.02 cm at 350 N, one bed) at 7.6 times the particles and 1.45 times the time. With 2 per edge, 250 iterations lower the sinkage further (0.85 cm with the 5 cm jitter, 0.60 cm at random in the sub-cell; one bed each) at 2.8 and 2.3 times the time: once the sampling is dense enough the iteration limit matters, as in the drag (P5, P6).
- **Not a matched material.** The 2-per-edge sand sinks 1.1 cm at 350 N where DEM A sinks 0.5 cm and the tuned 1 cm grid 0.2 cm, and it holds 700 N with 2.2 cm where DEM A gives way (4.8 cm). "Stops compacting" is the criterion here, not agreement with DEM.

**Stage 3: the four prescribed motions at these settings against DEM** (cases P-a, D-a, U-a, T-a of P7; three beds against the three DEM seeds; maps with the box cells left out):

| MPM setting | Push force [N], MPM | DEM | MPM / DEM | Push: trench depth [cm], MPM / DEM | Push: berm [cm], MPM / DEM | Push down: rim [cm], MPM / DEM | Push up: residual pit [cm], MPM / DEM | Twist: rim [cm], MPM / DEM | Displacement error [%], push / down / up / twist | DEM seed against seed [%] | Peak force while pressing, MPM / DEM |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Task settings (3 cm, 1.25 per edge), DEM at 733 kg/m3 | -88.0 | -79.4 | 1.11 | 8.3 / 6.3 | -0.3 / 2.3 | -0.1 / 1.1 | 4.6 / 4.6 | -0.1 / 0.9 | 47 / 70 / 91 / 68 | 16 / 20 / 38 / 23 | 0.43 |
| 3 cm, 2 per edge, jitter 5 cm, DEM at 640 kg/m3 | -119.6 | -70.7 | 1.69 | 8.6 / 6.2 | 1.7 / 2.1 | 0.4 / 1.1 | 4.9 / 4.6 | 0.8 / 1.0 | 37 / 50 / 89 / 43 | 15 / 17 / 41 / 21 | 1.19 |
| 3 cm, 2 per edge, random in sub-cell, DEM at 640 kg/m3 | -136.6 | -70.7 | 1.93 | 7.2 / 6.2 | 2.3 / 2.1 | 0.4 / 1.1 | 4.2 / 4.6 | 1.3 / 1.0 | 32 / 47 / 95 / 47 | 15 / 17 / 41 / 21 | 1.64 |
| Tuned (1 cm, 8 per cell, 1.67 ms, 250 iterations), DEM at 640 kg/m3 | -96.6 | -70.7 | 1.37 | 6.0 / 6.2 | 2.3 / 2.1 | 0.8 / 1.1 | 3.9 / 4.6 | 1.2 / 1.0 | 26 / 34 / 61 / 38 | 15 / 17 / 41 / 21 | 2.27 |

- **Rim: restored in the push and the twist.** With 2 per edge at random in the sub-cell the push berm is 2.3 cm against 2.1 cm in DEM and the twist rim 1.3 against 1.0 cm (task settings: -0.3 and -0.1 cm, no rim), both inside the range fixed beforehand (half to twice DEM). In the push downward the rim is 0.4 against 1.1 cm, below half of DEM. The trench of the push is 7.2 cm against 6.2 cm (task settings 8.3 cm).
- **Force agreement: lost.** The push force is -136.6 N against -70.7 N in DEM A, a ratio of 1.93 (1.69 with the 5 cm jitter), outside the 15% fixed beforehand; task settings give 1.11 and the tuned 1 cm grid 1.37. This is the voxel dependence of P6 seen from the other side: at a 3 cm voxel the well-sampled sand gave 2.08 times DEM there. So the agreement of the push force at task settings (+4 to +11%) belongs to the sand that compacts; the 3 cm sand that does not compact is about twice as strong as DEM A in the push.
- **Displacement** is closer to DEM than at task settings in the push, the push downward and the twist (32 / 47 / 47% against 47 / 70 / 68%) and not as close as the tuned 1 cm grid (26 / 34 / 38%).
- **Answer in one sentence.** The sparse particle sampling of the task (about 2 particles per 3 cm cell, scattered by a 5 cm jitter) is what makes the sand compact under a foot-sized load; 8 particles per cell placed one per sub-cell stops it at 3.9 times the particles and about 1.2 times the step time, restores the berm, and makes the sand about twice as strong as DEM A in the push, so it is not a drop-in replacement for the training setting.
- **Limits.** Stage 1 is one bed per setting. One box, loads up to 700 N (17.5 kPa; a G1 foot in single stance is about 29 kPa), one speed and depth per motion in stage 3. The step time is for one environment with every particle simulated; training uses many environments and the particle cache. No policy was trained on the new setting.

Figure: `site/T8/fig/r7_compaction.png`.

### P10. Videos of all motions (round 7, part B; website only; plan)

One clip per motion, MPM above DEM, real time (one frame per 0.04 s, 25 frames per second), the same framing and the same colour scale (speed) in both panels of a clip, the box drawn from its saved position. Side view of the sand within 3 cm of the centre plane for the horizontal push, the push downward, the push upward and the fixed-weight press at 350 N. For the twist the motion is in the horizontal plane, so the clip is a **top view** of the top 3 cm of the sand, with the box outline. Tuned MPM (1 cm) against DEM A at 640 kg/m3; a second set with task-setting MPM against DEM A at 733 kg/m3. The runs are repeats of cases P-a, S-a (which supplies the downward and the upward clip), T-a and the press at 350 N with the particles of the slab saved every 0.04 s. In the DEM press run for the video the force window is 0.01 s so that the load rises in 0.5 s as in MPM.

**Results of round 7, part B (checked with caveats by check 8).** Ten clips, each MPM above DEM (twist: MPM left, DEM right), real time (one frame per 0.04 s, 25 frames per second), the same framing and colour scale in both panels, the box drawn from its saved position. Originals tracked in `~/isaac/dem_compare/results/video/`; copies in `site/T8/media/` (ignored by git there). Script `analysis/video5.py`; the frames (sand within 3 cm of the centre plane, and the top 3 cm of the bed for the twist, with the speed of every particle or grain) were saved by the drivers with `--video_dt 0.04` in `t8-mpm-r7-video-1`, `t8-mpm-r7-videob-1` (one task rerun after a failure at setup) and `t8-dem-r7-video-1`, and downloaded to `data/r7_video`, `data/r7_dvideo` (untracked, 0.8 GB).

| Clip | View | Length | Colour scale (speed) | Tuned MPM (1 cm) against DEM A at 640 kg/m3 | Task-setting MPM (3 cm) against DEM A at 733 kg/m3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Horizontal push, 0.5 m/s at 6 cm (case P-a), from the lowering to the end | side | 3.8 s | 0 to 0.5 m/s | `push_tuned.mp4` | `push_task.mp4` |
| Push downward, 0.2 m/s to 6 cm, then held 0.5 s (first 1.3 s of case S-a) | side | 1.3 s | 0 to 0.2 m/s | `down_tuned.mp4` | `down_task.mp4` |
| Push upward, 0.2 m/s out of the sand (the rest of case S-a) | side | 1.3 s | 0 to 0.05 m/s | `up_tuned.mp4` | `up_task.mp4` |
| Twist, 90° at 1 rad/s at 6 cm (case T-a) | top, the top 3 cm of the bed | 3.4 s | 0 to 0.15 m/s | `twist_tuned.mp4` | `twist_task.mp4` |
| Fixed-weight press, 350 N, box free to sink | side | 3.0 s | 0 to 0.03 m/s | `press_tuned.mp4` | `press_task.mp4` |

- The clips are single runs (jitter seed 42 and DEM seed 42) made for viewing; no number in this file is taken from them.
- Reading aids, from check 8 (which watched the clips): in the push clips the MPM box trails the DEM box by 2 cm (gantry lag at 0.5 m/s); in the press and twist clips the DEM sand under the box is at full colour scale for a moment while the box is lowered, because the DEM zero lies 6.5 mm below the grain tops and the lowering presses them, while MPM stays still; in the twist clips white around the box is where the surface is more than 3 cm below its start, not empty space; in the press clips the load starts 1.0 s into the clip; the time in the title of the push-upward clips runs on from the downward clip; in `up_tuned.mp4` a thin sheet of MPM particles stays at the level of the former box underside across the pit, with a gap under it: these are the particles that sat inside the box faces during the press (the effect of P7, check 5), not sand bridging the pit.
- The twist is shown from above because its motion is in the horizontal plane; a side view of the centre plane would show almost nothing.
- In the DEM press run for the video the force window is 0.01 s, so the load rises in 0.5 s as in MPM (0.4 s in the runs of P8).
- What the frames I looked at show (one or two frames per clip, description and not measurement): in the push the tuned grid has a wedge of moving sand and a pile like those of DEM, task settings a smaller pile; after the push upward the pit wall has slumped in both tuned MPM and DEM; in the twist a ring of sand around the corners moves in both; in the press at task settings the box is about 6 cm down after 1.4 s under load while the DEM box has barely moved. The clips were not watched frame by frame.
- Each clip is 0.1 to 0.6 MB (H.264).

### Resolution, round 2 (T1 gantry; partly superseded by P5) (checked, with caveats)

**The MPM box forces of this section were recorded at one phase of a 20 ms cycle of the box speed and are superseded by P6 (round 4).** DEM forces, deformation, sand motion, density and tilt results are not affected.

Read with P5: every run of this section used the T1 gantry (1e5 N/m). The voxel series at task sampling and the iteration, time-step, margin and shift rows stand as measured on that gantry. The rows with more particles per cell or less jitter do not survive a stiff gantry (P5: -84.2 to -45.1 N at 4 to 1 cm with 8 particles per cell). In those runs the box carried 400 to 2088 N at the end of the hold and started the drag up to 2 cm short, but it was at depth in the window, so the shortfall is not shown to be the reason; why the soft gantry gives more force is not identified. Their forces (107 to 165 N) and the statements built on them are withdrawn.


Superseded by P6 (MPM force recorded at one phase): Round 2, T1 gantry, task sampling: the MPM force falls from -68 N at a 4 cm voxel to -34 N at 1 cm (factor 2.0; one run per point).

All runs below have the box underside 6 cm below the common surface (measured column), one run each.

- Box contact margin: set through the solver's collider material after the scene is built (printed back in the log). A negative margin shrinks the box, so a lower force is expected at any voxel size; that the same half-voxel shrink costs -35% at 4 cm and -15% at 2 cm is consistent with the box acting larger on a coarse grid, and does not prove it.
- Iteration cap: the run with cap 250 and tolerance 1e-6 takes about five times longer per step, so the standard runs stop at the cap of 25; the force changes by -1% and +4%. No record of residuals was obtained: setting the solver's `verbose` flag printed nothing to the job log.
- Bed shifted against the grid in z by 1/4, 1/2 and 3/4 voxel: +12 to +19% at 4 cm, -6 to +13% at 2 cm. Shifts in x and y were not run.
- Jitter: the task's jitter is a fixed 5 cm, which is 1.6 particle spacings at a 4 cm voxel and 6.3 at 1 cm. The beds with jitter 0.5 spacing or none at a 4 cm voxel keep their lattice layers, and the mass-equivalent surface is then sensitive to where its two planes fall (`checks/T8.md`, check 2: 0.2 to 2.9 cm for the jitter 0.5 run). The measured depth of that run is 6.36 cm, so its +71% and the -150 N without jitter are not at a well-defined equal depth. The 2 cm rows are less affected.
- Every row of this table and of the next is one run at one grid alignment.

Superseded by P6 (MPM force recorded at one phase): Round 2, T1 gantry: more particles per cell raised the force on the soft gantry, but not on a stiff one, where 8 particles per cell give -84 to -45 N at 4 to 1 cm (P5); these forces are withdrawn.

The MPM tilt test at voxel 3 cm with other samplings (rows "jitter" and "particles per cell edge" of the tilt tables in P1) shows that the sand itself changes: at voxel 3 cm with 2 and 3 particles per cell edge it no longer creeps and its first step above 1 cm/s is 40° and 42.5°; at voxel 4 cm with 3 per edge 42.5°, and with 5 per edge it does not slide up to 45° (0.16 cm/s at 45°). The task's sampling gives 32.5° with creep from 20°; the preset's friction coefficient corresponds to 36.1°. So the sparsely sampled sand is weaker than the preset and the densely sampled sand holds steeper slopes than the preset's friction angle in this walled bed; which of the two is closer to the continuum model was not determined.

- Small effects: iteration cap 250 with tolerance 1e-6 (-1% / +4% at 4 / 2 cm), time step 2.5 ms (+8% / +5%). Grid alignment: +12 to +19% and -6 to +13%. Box margin of minus half a voxel: -35% / -15%.
- With dense sampling the bed no longer compacts and holds slopes of 40 deg or more; that part is confirmed in P5 with the box at depth.
- Withdrawn: "well-sampled MPM gives 2.3 to 2.8 times the task-sampling force". Those forces (107 to 165 N) were measured on the soft T1 gantry, with the box at depth in the window; on a stiff gantry 8 particles per cell give -84 to -45 N at 4 to 1 cm (P5). Why the soft gantry gives more force is not identified.

**What the MPM force would be in the limit, and what it means for tasks at a 4 cm voxel.** Rewritten in round 3. No limit value can be given (P5): with the tuned setting and the box at depth the force is still falling at the finest grids run. For tasks: at task settings the force on this box is -68.4 N at 4 cm and -58.2 N at 3 cm on the T1 gantry and -40.0 and -34.3 N on a stiff gantry, against -84.2 and -64.5 N with the tuned sampling at the same voxels and -45.1 N at a tuned 1 cm voxel. So the task-setting sand is weaker than the same preset with 8 particles per cell at the same voxel (by a factor of 2.1 at 4 cm and 1.9 at 3 cm, stiff gantry on both sides), it compacts and creeps where the tuned sand does not, and its force on a body depends on how stiffly the body is held. These statements are about one 0.2 m box at 6 cm depth and 0.5 m/s; feet, other depths and speeds were not tested.

Figures: `site/T8/fig/resolution.png`, `sampling.png`, `causes.png`.

Floor layer: lowest 5 cm of the bed; wall strip: within 10 cm of the side walls; 99th percentile of particle speed at 1.0 m drag distance (box speed 50 cm/s). First-round checks that were not repeated at equal depth: a 3.0 x 1.6 m bed (force within 2% in both), a deeper DEM bed (+5%), stiffer DEM grains, half the DEM time step and restitution 0.5 (within 4%); see `results/round1/T8_round1.md`.

Video: `site/T8/media/drag_side_by_side.mp4` (not tracked by git there; original `~/isaac/dem_compare/results/drag_side_by_side.mp4`, tracked). Side view of the sand near the centre plane coloured by speed, MPM above DEM, box underside 6 cm below the common surface, 2.8 s at real time (71 frames at 25 per second, one per 2 cm of drag). Made by `analysis/video.py` from `t8-mpm-video-1` (task v03-video) and `t8-dem-video-1` (task A-r60-video), which repeat the main case of each simulator.

## Not done / caveats

- Neither simulator is the truth and there is no experiment here. DEM with 9 to 20 mm clumps is itself a model; its force also falls with grain size and has not levelled off.
- Round 5 was checked twice (check 5 found two results to be artefacts of the surface measure; check 6 passed the corrected version with wording changes, applied here). Three repeats per case, one tuned voxel (1 cm), one body, DEM material A only. The DEM seed-against-seed reference contains only the seed (not grain size or material) and is generous to MPM by about 1.4. The residual pit error of 25% is an upper bound: part of it is the DEM surface measure on the sloping pit wall. The push-with-twist case was run and is not reported.
- Round 6 (fixed-weight press) was checked once (check 7, pass with caveats; its corrections are applied). Open: which of particle sampling, iteration limit and time step causes the compaction under load at task settings; where the tuned sand gives way (1500 to 3000 N would be needed).
- Round 7 (P9, P10) was checked once (check 8, pass with caveats; its wording is applied). The non-sampling settings were each changed alone on one bed and never combined; no run is converged in the iteration limit. The 2-per-edge setting is not a matched sand and was not used for training.
- Round 4 was checked (check 4, pass with caveats); round 5 is unchecked. The corrected force is not converged in voxel size (-10% from 1.5 to 1 cm, -9 and -5% from 1 to 0.75 cm; no limit can be extrapolated), differs by up to 23% between grid alignments, changes by +4% with half the time step, and is not steady: it rises 5 to 7% across the window in MPM and 5% in DEM A at 733 kg/m3 (check 4).
- Every MPM box force of rounds 1 to 3 was recorded at one phase of a 20 ms cycle of the box speed and is kept only for the record: the task-setting forces (-59.4 and -34 N), the tuned series (-84 to -45 N), the depth, sampling and one-setting series, and the ratios DEM / MPM 1.37, 2.3 and 1.58. The gantry dependence reported in round 3 was this artefact. T1's box forces are affected in the same way.
- Not done with the corrected recording: the depth series, the particle-count and jitter variations, the round-2 sampling series, 0.75 cm on four alignments, the T1 gantry on four alignments, the lowering force. Not done at all: tuned MPM below 0.75 cm; a solver convergence record; the DEM tilt test at 640 kg/m3; other speeds, box sizes and materials. The DEM grain size is not converged either (15 to 11 mm: -71 to -63 N), so MPM / DEM at 1 cm is 1.34 to 1.49.
- Open from check 4: inside one step the sand solve appears to treat the box as a free 10 kg body, not as a body held by the gantry (read from the code, not tested there); the box-mass scan in P6 addresses it. The saved joint velocity reads 0.2 to 3.8% below the rate of the saved position; cause not identified, and which of the two the sand solver uses was not determined.
- The DEM material A was calibrated in round 1 to the task-setting MPM sand (slides at 32.5 deg by the 1 cm/s rule). Tuned MPM slides at 40 deg, nearer DEM material B (37.5 deg), which gives -99 N at 640 kg/m3 (one run): in the drag the tuned MPM is stronger than A at every voxel size and level with B at 1 cm (0.96). The only comparison in which the calibration holds is task settings against DEM A at 733 kg/m3 (1.09).
- Every point of the voxel, sampling, one-setting and depth series, the 9, 11 and 20 mm DEM runs and every MPM tilt run is a single run; the tuned voxel series is the mean of four runs per voxel size (four beds, four grid alignments).
- Rounds 1 to 3 were each checked by an independent agent (checks 1 to 3 in checks/T8.md); all three passed with caveats. Check 3 reproduced the round-3 forces, depths, deformation and tilt angles; it did not recompute the round-3 MPM densities and moving-sand numbers from particles. All runs were made in one session on 2026-11-24.
- The MPM box is a 10 kg body on a PD gantry (3e6 N/m in the tuned runs): at the end of the hold it carries 1345 to 2499 N and is 0.4 to 0.8 mm above its depth. As recorded at one phase, at 1 cm the vertical force falls from 2137 N to 17 N after 4 cm of drag and 14 N between 5 and 10 cm; at 2 cm, runs with 878 to 2271 N at the end of the hold give -59.8 to -62.4 N of drag force (within 2% of their mean). The DEM box is kinematic and carries 2 to 40 N; lowering is not a matched test.
- The MPM code is the stack archive of 2026-11-22 (IsaacLab a60612c79 with 69 uncommitted files, Newton e681453d), not the code of the T1 run and not today's tree.
- The local disk is full, so the round-3 particle dumps exist only in the OSMO output storage; the analysis ran there and its JSON output and logs are in results/.

Failures of the third round: the two 0.5 cm runs (out of memory), one 3 cm run (OSMO backend error, rerun), two gantry-damping variants that did not produce a valid drag. Failures of the second round: none of the 97 tasks failed. The solver residual printout produced no output. First-round failures are listed in `results/round1/T8_round1.md`.
